# Ratchet ledger (goal: ratcheting improvements; quality >= baseline; stop after >3 iterations of diminishing returns)
DO NOT DELETE: owned by the lead session. Agents cleaning scratch must leave cad-plan/ alone.

An iteration = one landed PR (or dropped item) with measured before/after. Diminishing = best primary-metric improvement < 5% AND no quality gain (bug fixed with regression test, skips removed, complexity/contract gain, reproducibility gain). Stop after 4 consecutive diminishing iterations. Timings count only from CI or host dev.
User update (17 Nov): hitting the 4-diminishing streak no longer stops work. After the streak, continue with every item still judged worth running (large measured or estimated gain, or a quality/contract gain); stop only proposing/measuring new low-ceiling candidates. The streak stays recorded for reporting.

## Method notes
- Comment-lint counts (`slopvac lint --mode code-comments`) count only when slopvac runs from inside the repo, with its config. Without the config the check is incomplete (exit 2) and its counts do not count. Record the exit code with each count.

## Quality floor (must never regress)
- Skill tests: all files pass; test count may only drop with a named replacement test (refactor PRs: tests unchanged).
- FreeCAD-gated tests run with 0 skips (CAD_REQUIRE_FREECAD=1).
- Harness tests + generate.py --check pass; scripts/typecheck.py passes.
- Sealed output bytes unchanged unless the PR declares a deliberate change.

Stated trade-off: the 3MF export also depends on the `material` domain, so editing a material profile property stales the 3MF even if the look does not change; a separate visual-material domain would avoid it at the cost of one more domain. No supplier order code is required. One parser serves the field and the title; `STANDARDS` covers DIN, ISO, EN, ASME, ANSI; a size is a pair, a metric or unified designator, a tolerance-class pair or a bare whole-token size, and `DIN 471` or `ISO 4762` alone identify nothing; a hardness number such as `200 HV` or a class such as `A2-70` is a grade, not a size. `THREADED_FASTENERS` (ISO 4762, 4014, 4017, 7380, 10642, 4032, DIN 912, 933, 934) require a thread designation, so `ISO 4762 - 8.8` fails; `FEATURE_STANDARDS` (ISO 965, 261, 262, 68, 286, 2768, 1101, ASME Y14.5, B1.1) are not purchasable parts, so a designation of only those needs an MPN, matched by body plus number so `DIN 965 M5x12` stays a screw. The check runs only for parts whose route has a `purchased` operation; a made-to-drawing CNC part gets no `purchase.*` obligation (pinned by a test). A strip anchored on its two long sides, three sides or four sides gives its width; a strip anchored on its two short ends only gives its length; one anchor, or anchors that only touch, is an `overhang`; anything else (L, ring, trapezoid, notch, boss, hole, concave or non-tangent or oversize arc, non-circular cap) gives `bridge` with an unknown span. Cost of the rule, stated by the PR: stadium roofs with a hole or a notch in a round end moved from a number to unknown (`pocket_stadium_hole`, `pocket_stadium_notch`); no corpus case moves across the four FDM families (34 pair runs). Fallback fix after the #180 diagnosis (a detection downgrade found at `db03d70`): a flat 90 degree ceiling that is not a strip used to drop from a major FAIL to a minor UNKNOWN; a bridge is now judged by span only when it has a measured span, and every other face, including `span_mm` None, goes through the overhang-angle test, so 90 degrees still fails; new pair `ring_ceiling` (mutant: a flat ring ceiling; twin: a 45 degree V roof) in `additive_support_overhang_bridge`. Three new error codes: `evidence.scratch_path`, `evidence.uncited_artifact` (the text cites a record result that `artifacts` omits) and `evidence.refused_dependency` (the entry names another evidence id that the record refuses; the reliance is transitive and runs to a fixed point, so a cycle of accepted entries stays accepted and a cycle with one refused member refuses both). A path counts as inside when it resolves within the project root after `..` and symlinks and git tracks it or it sits in a record-managed result location (a sealed `hw/versions/v<N>/` or a bundle kind in `layout.json`); refused are `.cad-work/`, `hw/.cad/`, temp directories, `~/`, anything outside the root, an untracked file in a git project and a file that does not exist. Fields are skipped by exact schema pointer, never by key name, and prose fields are scanned for path-shaped tokens (a scratch prefix, an absolute path, a `..` segment, or a file name after a `/` with a non-numeric stem and a 1 to 8 character extension containing a letter); structured paths (`artifacts[].path`, `bundle.path`, `source.artifact` and similar) go through the inside rule. LIMIT stated by the PR: an entry that states measured figures with no path and no entry id in its text raises no finding, because the rule has no signal there; the narrower rule needs layout roles per file and a number-and-unit match, and is not implemented. STATED LIMITS: `--committed` skips the threshold-raise comparison (`require_threshold_kept`), because HEAD compared with itself could never fail, and it cannot see history, so a threshold raised in an earlier commit that left `flow.stage` below it prints `required: false` and passes; run the pre-commit hook or the staged `--verify` on the commit that edits `flow.recheck_from`. The layout audit still reads the work tree, so run it on a clean checkout. Every other check runs against HEAD in both modes (stamp stage, stamp below the committed threshold, `passed`, tree hash, record and evidence hashes, no blocking findings in the packet). [#159](https://github.com/benchhand/benchhand/issues/159) is now closed by #208 (row 99), which adds the CI verify step with `--committed`. Comment lint re-run by the clerk from detached checkouts at the repo root (`01b7b7b` and `ff2b715`), no unchecked rules: `agreement.py`, `agreement_probe.py` and `test_agreement.py` 0 -> 0, the new `ring.py` and `check_with_control_off.py` 0, `test_cad_model.py` 95 -> 95 (the PR's table says 89 -> 89 against its older base; row 96 explains the 89 -> 95) | `reference_deviation(shape, linear_mm)` meshes a copy of the shape the way the agreement meshes its reference, takes the 8 largest triangles of each measured face and returns the largest exact distance from their centers to the face, with the face index and surface type; `occurrences` judges an occurrence `unknown` when that distance exceeds the threshold `REACH_MARGIN x REFERENCE_FACTOR x linear` = 2 x 0.5 x 0.05 = 0.05 mm, so a clean part's GLB error (at most 0.05 mm) plus a reference at the threshold (at most 0.05 mm) is 0.10 mm, under the 0.155 mm reach with a 1.55x margin; the reason names the part, the face (index and type), the error and the threshold, and the row flows into `abstentions` without blocking. It measures every face except planar faces and cylinders (a flat extrusion counts as planar; a cylinder's chord sagitta was measured at most 0.0111 mm over radii R0.5 to R46.05 mm and angles 0.25 and 0.1 rad). `occurrences` accepts an optional `reference_budget_mm` for tests. STATED LIMIT: the sample (8 largest triangles per face, centers only) gives no upper bound; it catches a mesher that misses its deflection across a face, as an uncontrolled torus does, and can miss a local overshoot. Correction of the record by the PR: an earlier measurement of the reference mesh's error, in the #138 discussion, measured against the whole solid (a center inside a convex solid reads 0) and reported 0.0242 mm; the corrected exact per-face distances at 0.025 mm linear and 0.25 rad are 0.0787 mm (LMP-0004 face 28, default mesher) and 0.0202 mm (flag on), with the largest on lamp-arm 0.0302 mm (LMP-0001 face 10); #76's margins (row 85) stand because they use nearest-triangle distances between the two meshes. Cause read from the code, not proven by a run: the test overwrites an interned object with other bytes of the same length (`geometrX`), and `Store._checked` (cad-record `store.py:133`, `:166-168`) caches the verification result by the signature `(st_ino, st_size, st_mtime_ns)`, so a same-size rewrite within the file system's timestamp step keeps the old signature and the record still reads as intact. It is the flake class #93's PR text names for `test_schema_registry` ("same-length edit that keeps file times"). CI passed on the PR head, so the flake is timing dependent. A user-visible consequence, if the diagnosis holds: a same-size in-place edit of an object within one timestamp step is not detected by a `Store` instance that checked it before; I did not test that outside the test. | the store replaces the part store at the same layout ids: `cache.part_objects` and `cache.part_records` now point at `hw/.cad/cache/objects/{SHA}` and `records/{SHA}.json`, with new `cache.locks` and `cache.findings`, and the old paths become `cache.former_part_objects` and `..._records` so an existing project audits clean until `store gc`. `store.py` (standard library only) offers `intern` (once under its sha256, read-only, outputs become links), `place_object`, `place` (a multi-file record as a folder, whole or not at all, atomic swap on Linux `renameat2` and macOS `renamex_np`), `record` (re-hashes every member on read, returns a `miss:` or `stale:` reason), `claim` (under a per-key `flock`; the first usable record wins, a differing second writer is a finding), `retire`, `lock` and `gc` (keeps every member of any sealed manifest under `hw/`, and live records; removes the rest and the former `hw/.cad/parts/` and `builds/`). `layout.py` gains `input_key` (refuses a timing such as `build_s`), `code_shas` (no caller yet; the mesh cache is next) and `tool_versions`. SEALED OUTPUTS: no sealed output or manifest format changes; part keys are byte-identical to main (fixture `part_keys_main.json` from `074280f`, `test_every_lamp_arm_part_key_is_byte_unchanged` matches 11 of 11, and 11 of 11 on the dev builds, PR-reported); manufacturing keys differed from main `f7caee8` in the dev run (0 of 7), which the PR explains by that main's absolute-path module identity versus #177's skills-relative `code_shas` closure (7 of 7 stable within each tree), with `OUTPUTS_FORMAT` unchanged. No Markdown changed, so no prose lint | `fea_stress.py` (standard library only; no FreeCAD, `fea_static` or `fea_conditions`) reads node coordinates, von Mises stress and displacement from a ccx `.frd`, finds with a grid hash the nodes farther than 3 x h_coarse from every fixed-face and load-face node (the same distance for both meshes) and compares the kept peaks. `settled`: interior peak, stress change at most 2%, displacement change at most 2%, hot spot moved at most h_coarse; reports the larger kept peak and is strength-eligible. `settled_away_from_support`: peak within h_coarse of the exclusion boundary, every other criterion met, position not compared; information only, not strength-eligible, so strength stays UNKNOWN until #209. UNKNOWN (no number) for a change above 2%, a hot spot moved more than h_coarse, a peak near a fixed or load face, a kept region under the floor (1000 nodes on the fine mesh, 10% share on both), a high-order fallback that differs between meshes, or a non-finite value; the message names both h values, both peaks, the change, the hot spot, the nearest face and the kept share. An unterminated `.frd` block raises `FrdError`, and the later PR2 caller must map it to UNKNOWN. The guard against a peak near a face only fires for K < 1, so a test lowers K to reach it. Decision per the latest comment on #191: option (b) with a safeguard, sharp re-entrant corners not excluded, support- and load-governed parts go to #209. Now it takes an `flock` on the target folder, swaps a fresh link onto the target (Linux `renameat2` RENAME_EXCHANGE, macOS `renamex_np` RENAME_SWAP), hashes the renamed inode through the open descriptor, and returns True only after checking the target still names that inode; on a wrong hash or exception it undoes the swap and removes only its own link. A file kept because a newer file holds the target moves to `hw/.cad/cache/recovery/` with a `kept:` line in `cache.findings`; `Store.remove_tree()` skips a pinned inode, and the cad-model build and manufacture cleanups call it. Without the rename flags `place_object()` returns False and cad-model builds the part.
